lion men. Besides, with her political afiairs not going along as'

Yet, Chiang Kai-shek, relying upon tho assistance frcm England and

is a great country and is unlike Europe. Thereforo, even if the

Talk by Noboru Imao, Yokohama branch manager of Tokyo
Nichi-Nichi, entitled "Returning From America."
Jepanese residents of America, how are you? I cannot say
more than express my hearty ap reciation for your kindness during
my visit there. Through the kindness of the Tokyo Broadcasting
Station, it has become possible for me to talk to you tonight and
I would like to take adventage of this opportunity to express my
thanks. Only e month (may be seven months, for ichi, which means
one, is similer to the sound of shichi, 'which means seven) has
pessed since I bede farewell to you all, but a great change has
taken place in the world situation. Germany and Russia are now
actually at war with each other and America has taken a step forward
tow d participation in the war with her occupation of Iceland.
Consequently, I believe that you all are somewhat worried. How-
ever, since I have clreedy tslked of this thing during my lecture
tour while abroad, I bolieve that there is no need to be surprised.
In as much as America, & nation as large as a mountain and as wide
as a sea, has started moving towerd a certain definite direction,
it is impossible for her to change her course. Consequently, you
must all be prepared beforehand to be able to face the fact thet
Americe will take e step further and finally take part in the Euro-
pean War, both methodically and materially. hen the relations
between Germany and Soviet Russie prior to the war are considered,
it can be seen that they would have to fight &gainst each other
sooner or kter. Thus, tho world, today, is being manipuleted by
the stringw of fate and is fighting, regardless of the welfare of
humanity. Under this world situation, wo Japanese need only cnn-
sider how wo can alweys livo strongly and uprightly. No matter
how upright one may bo he cannot hope to win against one who is
evil and strong. However, if ono is upright and very strong, he
has nothing to fear, no mattor what situation he is confronted with.
This, I think, is the montel ettitude that should not be forgotten
in order to accomplish the greet ideal of tho Japenese race--that
of constructing a ndw order in East Asia and thoroby establishing
a mutuel prosperity sphero in East Asic and, in thc long run, pre-
serving the pecce of humanity. Although it has been stated re-
peatedly, I would like to mention the fect that Jap n is the land
of the Gods and that wo, Japanese, are all children of the Gods.
Thus, there is no reason why Japan, which has been founded by the
wishos of tho Gods and which is acting in. accordance with the order
of the Gods, should not receive the divine protection of tho Gods.

This is the reason why we can entertain an undescribable peace of
mind. Therefore, no matter how the world situation of America's
movement may change; you Japanese residents of abroad, who are
children ofthe Ccds, need not worry in the least bit. When I re-
turned to Japan after an absence of about five months, I saw Japan
burning with a powerful mental power or devine power--that is,
a powerful spirit capable of overcoming any difficulty. When I
saw this situation, I wes very happy. With this vitality and
spirit, there is nothing that cannot be done. I believe that it
is not a matter of time or how we think, for when we consider
the hardships and difficulties experionced by Emperor Jimmu when
he came down from Heaven some 2,6oo years ago, the test assigned
to us today is nothing. Instead, I think that we should suffer
and be harassed more. This is hecause it is said that the greater
the birth pains, the creater vill be the thing tht will be born.
We should refreshen in our minds the groat spirit of subjugation
(?) of Emperor Jirmu to the very end and carry out the great mis-
sion assigned to the Yamato race with the spirit of the officers
and men, who have been serving in North, Contral and South China
ever since the cutbreak of the incident, as our spirit. Recently
Premier Konoye stated in regard to Japan's attitude to cope with
the complicated and delicate international situation that Japan
will follow the co irse that sho should take. Therefore, all we
need to do is to follow the course that should be taken by Japan,
and Japan should follow the course that should be takon by her
to the vory ond. Japen's move, thus, hes becomo the target of
the attontion of tho world. Consequently, the responsibility of
you, Japanese residents of abroad, is greator than that of the
Japanese rosiding in Jepan. Since cach and evory move of you
will seriously affect the world rclations, to say nothing of tho
Japenese-American relations, I sincerely hope that you will be
more careful of your acts in the futuro.

Talk by Yoshinori Miyauchi, head of the harbor commission
of Osaka, on "Osaka Harbor".
The speaker gave the historical development of Osaka
Harbor (created in July, 1868). Primarily used as a port for
domestic trade to date. Is gradually teking on the aspects of
a port for domestic trade to date. Is gradually taking on the
aspects of a port for international trade. With the establish-
ment of a new order in East Asia, Osaka Harbor is destined to
take on greater importance.

Good evening, children of Hawaii. Are you all studying
hard? The children of Tokyo are eagerly looking forward to the
Sunmer vacation and are playing and studying very hard. How are
you going to spend this Summer vacation? As for the children of
Tokyo, the most pleasant thing for them to do during Sunmer is
swimming. All the rivers swelling and flowing near Tokyo, not .
to mention the pools withi Tokyo, will be filled with children,
however, children, whose homes are far way , will return to their
native place with their parents and spend their Summer in the
beloved mountains and rivers of their native place, thus building
up their soul and body. As you all know, four years have passed
since the outbreak of the current incident end when we think of
the soldiers who are fighting on the continent of China, we fool
that it si not justifiable for the children to spend their Summer
in idleness. Consequently, some schools are requiring children
to attend school even during the Summer months not for the purpose
of studying, but to perform radio exercises, visit nearby shrines
and pray for the success of the officers and ren fighting in China.
In regard to the Summer vacation of the children of Emergency
Japan, we will broadcast some cheerful news from time to time.
Now, iet me tell you about the completion of the first undersea
tunnel for trains to be made in the world. If you will see an
atlas of Japan, you will be able to readily find the place. There
is a big island west of Japan Proper called Kyushu. From Moji,
situated at the northern tip of this island, an undersea tunnel
has been constructed to Shimonoseki, a city five kilometers across
the sea on the island of Japan Proper. Furthermore, the comple-
tion ceremony of the great undersea tunnel for train transportation
was held on July 10. The construction of this tunnel was started
five years ago, and the number of mon that worked on this project
was 1,900,00o. Thore is an under-water tunnel under the Hudson
River of A.erica, but above this Kammon Tunnel which was recently
completed in Japan, the rough waves of the sea are surging. Plans
for the construction of undersea tunnels at Dover Strait and Gib-
raltar in Europe were considerod, but no definite action has been
taken to date. The fact that Japan has successfully constructed
this undersea tunnel in spito of the incouveniences caused by tho
war is certeinly pregnant with a great significance. The only
work left is to enlarge the tunnel end aly down tracks. Thorefore
by October of next year, tho first undersea tunnel for train will
meke its appearanco. How wonderful is it that the droam of trains
running under the sea has been realized! As you all know Germany
and Russia, at present, is ongeged in a croat war along the bordor
of Germany end Russian. Hitlor of Germany reportodly has gone to
take charge of the German High Command at the front.

Now, wouldn't it be of interest to you to know what Hitler is do-
ing at the front? Hitler is a very healthy man and reportedly

Good evenins, Children of Hawaii. Tlis is you long
awaited Children's News Hour From Tokyo, Japan. Today is July
19th in Jepan, which is a day earlier than Hawaii. In Japan,
July 13 to 16 was celebrated as the Bon festival, which is one
of the old time customs of Jepan. We believe that you fathers
and mothers, who were born in Japan, fondly recalled this festival.
In Japan, it is believed that the departed souls of our ancestors
return to their respective homes during this period. Consequently
to welcome the souls of our ancestors home fire is lighted in
front of homes throuthout Japan. This fire is called "Mukai-bi"
or welcoming fire. Within the homes are laid straw mats, upon
which are placed tables with offerings for the departed souls
of our ancestors. Together with these offerings are placed melons
and egg-plants, made in the forms of animals. It is always be-
lécved that the departed souls of our ancestors ride away on
these animels made from molons and egg-plants. Isn't this a beau-
tiful custom, somewhat like a fariy tale. Perhaps, some of your
parents celebrated the Bon Festival.

=== Page 46 === (confidence 0.99)
Page #3
Ever since the three Bon Festival periods, Tokyo has been exper-
iencing real summer with the sun shining brightly every day.
However, due to the intermitten rain, it is not so hot yet. On
the other hand, all the pools of Tokyo have been opened and are
being crowded with children after school hours every day. Now,
let me explain to you the extablishment of the sea holiday. As
you all know, Japan is an island empore, bordered on the north
by the Japan See and on the south by the Pacific, which is rich
in natural resources and by way of which traffic is maintained
with America as well as Manchoukuo and China by boat. Thus, it
can be said that the sea is Japan's freight (?). Therefore,
Japan has decided to designate July 20 as sea holiday and hold
various celebrations pertaining to the sea, thus deepening the
'nowledge of the nation in regard to the sea. In connection with
this sea holiday, a Kaiyo Kojo (Literally means gym) will be er-
tablished in Tokyo Harbor, which is the gate-way of Tokyo, for
the children to make them associate vith the sea more intimately
for the purpose of building up their bodies. This gym will be
constructed on a ten acre reclaimed land in the form of a ship
three stories high. Besides, a yacht harbor will be extablished,
where boats of all sized will be kept ofr the use of the children
of Tokyo at all times, thus civing them sea training. This is
being looked forward eagerly by the children. In closing, we
believe that you have heard much from your par ats and your
elder brothers and sisters in regard to the Russo-German war.
As you all know, Germany and Russia are engaged in a great war,
stretching from the Baltic Sea on the north to the Black Sea on
the'south. This war front is approximately 2,O00 kilometers long.
Now: in regard to the war front of the Japanese troops fighting
in Chine against Chaing Kai-shek, dont be surprised, for it it
is 5,000 kilometers bng, stretching from 45 degrees north latitude
in Mongolia on the north 21 degroes north latitude in French Indo-
China &nd Hainan Island on tho south.Thus, it is approximatoly
two and a half timos greater than the Russo-German war front.
Besides, in regard to the climate, there is no great differonce
in the climate along the Russo-German war front, but along the
war front in China, there is a great difference. In Mongolia on
the north, the climato is 40 degrees below zero in win: or and
120 degrees in the surmer. Besides, in contrast to the numerous
mountains on the continent, the Ru so-German war is being conducted
on a great lovel plain. By these facts, you can seo the haraships
experienced by cu Japanoso troops in China. Well, good-night
children of Hawaii.
Nursery Tunos.

News of Japan for the month of July by Lt. General
Sakurai, (post not definite.)
Although you, Japanese residents abroad, are far
away from your fatherland, you all must be worrying how the war
(China incident) is progressing day and night. Fou years of
war, that certainly is not a short war, but we are not fighting
for ourselves, but for those of ten or more years hence. If we
were to conclude the war in a half-baked manner, there might be
another war. We must win the war, and the time will come when
even the people of China (?) will come to appreciate our effort.
In the wars of today, there are no first lines, nor second lines,
nor third lines. Those on the second line are also people of the
first line, together with the soldiers et the front.

=== Page 54 === (confidence 0.99)
Pa€e#4
You, Japanese residing abroad, too, are people of the front line.
Some of youumay be thinking that Japan is suffering a shortage
of materials and you may have heard many false propaganda, but
what we are experiencing today is only proper in view of the four
years of war. But this is not true, for we are preserving our
food-stuffs, and this is a very reasonable measure. (This part
very indistinct) Where can we find a more bles ed nation than
Japan? How was the condition of the nations that fought for five
years during the previous World Var? (This part very indistinct)
Through the help of young boys and gins, the families of the sold-
iers at the front have been able to complete their rice planting.
They are doing all sorts of national service and are not playing
because it is hot. They are trying to build up their bodies and
are continuing their studies. I presume that this is also ture
of you all (Indistinct) Where there is since eity the masses
will gather. Thus, we are today fighting for the sake of sin-
serity. Therefore, I believe that t. e day will certainly come
when China (?) wili express her appreciate toward us. Japanese
residents abroad, please remember that your actions and speech
will have a great effect upon our country. Therefore, we should
be very careful. In view of the times, I believe that you Japa-
nese residents abroad should be all the more cautious. Summer
has come, but but Japan is burning with a great ambition and
spirit. Japanese residents residing so far, far away, I sin-
cerely hope for your success.

Comments on Current Events:
To talk on "The Chugen Operation of the Japanese Troops":
The Japanese troops' pperation in the southern part of Shansi
Province is now in its final stage with the Japanese troops mop-
ping up the remnants of the Chinese troops. In regard to this
operation, the press section of the Imperil Military Headquarters
issued the following announce:ent on June 4th: "Our troops
started their offensive on May 7th and defeated the 200,000
strong Chinese troons, annihilating half in doing so." I would
now like to talk about the significance and effect of this opera-
tion to the future developments. According to the announcement
made by the pr ss section of the Imperiel Military Headquarters,
the Chinese 1eft 50,000 bodies on the field, while 25,000 others
were captured, besides nuerous field crtilleries, machine cuns,
rifles and amrinitions. In other words, including the captives
and the deed left on the field, the Chinese suffered a casualty
of about l00,o00. This achievement vas accomplished by our troops
in only twenty days. This shows how fast the athack of the Japa-
nese troops is and also how strong the Japanese troops are. In
regerd to how seriously the Chungking Government has been affec-
ted by this defeat, it can be seen by the fact that she has lost
the greater part of her main ermy and, at the same time, she has
lost a region very rich in natural resources. To begin with,
this region had been under the protecticn of the 200,000 troops
under General Orikko end hed been favored vith impregnable na-
tural barriers. However, our troops overcame these natural ar-
riers in twenty days and ceptured or killed many leaders of the
Chinese troops, totalling about 85 in all. Because of this, many
of the Chinese soldiers lost their spirit to fisht and surrendered.
Due to the region being vory mountainous, our troops encountered
considerable difficultios, being unable to use its mechanized
twoops. In somo instances, food-stufis were dropped from planes
because of lack of cood roads. In spite of these difficulties,
our troops acconplished its ain. An interosting story in con-
nection with this oporation is our troops' capture of 1,500
Chinese troops with herd bread. That is, when our troops start-
ed feeding the captured Chinose, about 1,500 Chineso troops,
who were hiding in the mountains, saw this and unable to with-
stand hunger eny longer, thoy cano out and surrendered to the
Jepanoso troops.

Sir:
I have the honor to transmit herewith a copy of
C
1
1/
a note verbale dated June 28, 1941, from the British
Embassy to the Japanese Ministry of Foreign Affairs
protesting against the "unfair and subversive" nature
of broadcasts from Japanese radio stations directed
at audiences in India and Burma. The note verbale
reviews previous representations made on this subject
since July 1940, cites specific instances of the
broadcasts
(1

=== Page 128 === (confidence 1.00)
-2-
broadcasts complained about, which are described as
"undisguised German propaganda combined with sustained
attempts to undermine the loyalty" of the peoples of
the British territories concerned, and requests that the
strongest measures be taken to put a stop to the pro-
ceedings.
The note further states that news broadcasts in
Japanese for Japanese hearers are consistently unfriendly
to Great Britain and hopes that in this case also
"steps will be taken to ensure such broadcasts from
Tokyo may be so framed as to serve Japanese rather
than German interests."
Respectfully yours,
Joseph C. Grew

=== Page 129 === (confidence 1.00)
Enclosure no. 1 to despatch
no. 5727 dated July 17, 1941,
from the Embassy at Tokyo
(With the compliments of the British Embassy to the
American Embassy, received July 8, 1941)
The British Embassy to the Japanese Ministry of Foreign Affairs
No. 120(35/665/41)
NOTE VERBALE
His Majesty's Embassy present their compliments
to the Ministry of Foreicn Affairs and have the honour
to bring to the Ministry's notice the matter of the
unfair and subversive nature of radio broadcasts especially
directed at audiences in British territory which are
frequently emitted by the Japanese broadcasting station.
2. The matter was first brought to the attention
of the Ministry on July 25th, 1940 when, on information
from the Government or India, Mr. H. N. Brain of His
Majesty's Embassy drew the attention of Mr. Hori of the
Information Department to the fact that the news bulle-
tins in Hindustani broadcast from Japan especially for
audiences in India contained references to "British
tyranny in India" and other objectionable anti-British
material. A specific broadcast of an anti-British
nature was brought to Mr. Hori's attention on August
28th. It is understood that after these conversations
the tone of the broadcasts in question became for a
time much more objective.
3. On the 24th October 1940, however, it became
necessary for Mr. Brain to leave with Mr. Kabayama of
the Information Department of the Ministry a paper in which
it was stated "Complaints have been received from the
British authorities in Burma regarding certain objection-
able features of broadcasts from Tokyo in Burmese, which
appear

=== Page 130 === (confidence 0.99)
-2
appear to be designed to undermine the morale and
loyalty of the Burmese people. First place is habitually
given to enemy news about the war at the expense of
British news and the announcer concluded the broadcast
on the 3rd October with a slogan which is associated
with subversive political activity in Burma and which
means roughly. 'Up the Revolution!'
4. In January 194l, after receipt of information
from the Government of Burma that the Tokyo broadcast
in Burma had become a vehicle for German propaganda, Mr.
Brain again brought the subject to the notice of Mr.
Kabayama and warned him that, if there was no improvement
in broadcasts directed towards the Burmese people in
their own language, the reception or dissemination of
Japanese broadcasts in Burma might have to be prohibited.
5. On each of these occasions Mr. Kabayama promised
to look into the matter but, while his efforts are appre-
ciated, it is regretted that no improvement took place
in the broadcasts. On March 1st, 2nd and 3rd, the Tokyo
broadcasts in the Burmese language rose to peaks of anti-
British virulence not hitherto attained. The following
were the main characteristics:
(A) Gloating prominence was given to Axis war news
about alleged British misfortunes and failures.
(B) Complete silence was maintained regarding
British successes.
(C) Allegations were made about the stationing of
Chinese troops in Burma, accompanied by such remarks as
"mean actions of the British", "dirty scheme".
(D)

=== Page 131 === (confidence 0.99)
-3-
(D) Warnings were given that British interference
in the Sino-Japanese hostilities would bring suffering
to innocent Burmese people.
(E) The weakness of the British was said to be
shown by their reliance on Chinese troops.
(F) There was a complacent anticipation of British
disaster when the new Germen blockade by U-boat and
aircraft should begin.
6. The points described hitherto were brought to
the attention of Mr. Tashiro, head of the Information
Bureau, by the Counsellor of H.M.Embassy, on the 19th
March, 194l, and the f ormer was good enough to undertake
a careful enquiry into the matter. Subsequently on
March 28th Mr. Kabayame informed Mr. Brain that the
Broadcasting Corporation had been informed of the complaint
and that "slogans and catchwords of violent cheracter
would disappear." At the end of May, however, information
was received from the Government of Burma that, despite
the intervention of the Imperial Ministry of Foreign
Affeirs, the Tokyo broadosts in Burmese continued to be
as objectionable as ever. The output was described as
undisguised German propaganda combined with sustained
attempts to undermine the loyalty of the Burmese people.
7. Nor have these objectionable propaganda broad-
casts from Tokyo been confined to broadcasts in the
Burmese language directed to Burma or in Hindustani di-
rected to India. The following is a summary of the
broadcast at O540 G.M.T. (1440 Tokyo time) on June 11th
in

=== Page 132 === (confidence 0.99)
-4-
in Arabic directed to Muslim listeners throughout the world,
of whom there are many in India.
"Japan regards the British invasion of Syria as
unreasonable and based on the evil design of capturing
petrol dumps of Syria and Mosul. rance is striving to
check the aggressor. Japan fully recognises the inde-
pendence of Syria and hopes that Syria will be a member
of new world order". The broadcast then went on to exhort
all Arabs who are "suffering at the hands of the British"
to "free themselves from the British tyrants and make
their sole objective to see the flag of British im-
perialism lowered". It characterised British history as
an embodiment of injustice and aggression" and added
that no secrifice was too great for the cause in question.
"Do or die, God is with you".
8. Further, during the month of April 194l ob-
jectionable propaganda broadcasts were directed in the
Melay language towards the people of Malaya. It was
stated on various occasions that a German victory in
the European War was certain and that Anglo-American
promises of assistance to all countries were empty. It
was also asserted that "The Greek Prime Minister committed
suicide because he had been let down by the British"
and hearers were warned that the "British would only
betray them in a crisis".
9. It thus appears that over the period in question,
the occurrence of German propaganda broadcasts has not
been occasional or incidental but has been frequent and
apparently part of a policy puraued by the Tokyo broad-
casting authorities in the choice of broadcasts in the
languages

=== Page 133 === (confidence 0.99)
-5-
languages of peoples in British territory. H.M.Embassy
are at a loss to understand how the Japanese Government
can permit the issue to such peoples by the representative
Japanese broadcasting station of broadcasts which are
nothing more nor less than German propaganda, for which,
it is presumed, the Japanese Government do not wish to
be held responsible. It is clear in this connexion that
the source of such material is irrelevant, whether it be
sensational newspaper reports or the Broadcesting
Corporation's own sources of information; the fact
remains that by the constant use of it the Corporation
is making itself the vehicle of German propaganda.
H.M.Embassy must emphasize again that these talks and
other broadcasts referred are directed specifically at
audiences in British territory for whom the subject
matter has been carefully chosen and in whose language
they are couched. H.M.Embassy must request in the cir-
cumstances that the strongest measures be taken to put
a stop to these proceedings.
10. H.M.Embassy would also remind the Ministry
that the news broadcasts in Japanese for Japanese hearers
are consistently in a form preponderantly unfriendly to
Great Britain, and would express the hope that, in this
case also, steps will be taken to ensure such broadcasts
from Tokyo may be so framed as to serve Japanese rather
than German interests.
BRITISH EMBASSY,
TOKYO.
June 28th, 1941.

Den Mittelpunkt der Sendungen bildet also der Nachrich-

tendienst, der in jeder Sprache 15 Minuten lang durchgeführt
hören Sie auch fast jeden Tag europäische Musik, die jedoch
wird. Sie hören darin nicht nur von den wichtigen Ereignissen
zum grossen Teil von japanischen Künstlern ausgeführt wird.
in Ostasien, sondern auch über die japanischen Pressestimmen
Über deren Auffassung bezw. Wiedergabe der abendländi-
oder die Ansichten der japanischen Oeffentlichkeit zu wichtigen
schen Meisterwerke der Tonkunst erhalten wir seit Jahren
weltpolitischen Geschehnissen. Besonders unsere deutschen
ständig anerkennende Zuschriften von dortigen Hörern. Auf
Bundesgenossen dürften nicht versäumen, sich unsere Mel-
dem Gebiete der Symphonie und sonstigen grossen Orchester-
dungen anzuhören, da unsere beiden Länder gemeinsame In-
werke sind wir besonders auf die Darbietungen unseres
teressen verfolgen. Für Sie wäre schliesslich besonders
Rundfunk-Symphonie-Orchesters stolz.—Wie oben angegeben,
interessant, darüber zu hören. wie Ihre Volksgenossen in
verkünden wir an jedem Freitag um 21.25 Uhr das Programm
Japan leben oder wie die innige Freundschaft zwischen den
der folgenden Woche im voraus.
beiden Völken, Japanern und Deutschen, in den verschiedenen
Zum Schluss möchten wir uns an unsere Hörer mit einer
Veranstaltungen ihren Ausdruck findet. Solche Nachrichten
Bitte wenden. Wir haben nämlich grosses Interesse dafür,
bringen wir besonders häufig. Wir möchten Sie ausserdem
wie unsere Sendungen von Ihnen empfangen werden, ob
auf unsern Vortragsdienst aufmerksam machen. Im oben
unsere Programme Ihnen gefallen usw. Wir sind daher stets
angegebenen Programm finden Sie zwei verschiedene Bezeich-
sehr dankbar für Berichte und Anregungen Ihrerseits und
nungen: Vorträge und Sondervorträge. Unter Vorträgen
bitten Sie, möglichst ausführlich Ihre Empfangsverhältnisse
sind diejenigen gemeint, die von in Japan lebenden Deutschen
anzugeben, besonders die Namen unserer Sender, Wellenlänge,
oder deutschen Besuchern oder auch deutschsprechenden
Japanern über besondere kulturelle und sonstige Themen
Frequenz, Sendezeit, Inhalt der Sendung usw. Unsere Adresse
finden Sie unten angegeben.
gehalten werden. Dagegen werden die Sondervorträge von
Vorliegende Druckschrift, welche monatlich erscheint, erhält
den Mitarbeitern unserer Gesellschaft selbst verfasst. Sie
jeder auf Verlangen kostenlos zugesandt.
behandeln hauptsächlich -aktuelle Probleme, aber auch kul-
turelle Themata. Am Donnerstag bringen wir gewöhnlich

Ampliación de Nuestra Trasmisión
Se ha estado llevando a cabo desde el comienzo del presente año una gran ampliación de nuestra
trasmisión a ultramar, incluso la transmisión a Sudamérica.
Estamos trasmitiendo a trece direcciones durante cuarenta y una horas al dia, empleando dieciseis
idiomas inclusive el españo! y el portugués.
Lamentamos no disponer todavia de los detalles del programa y nos permitimos llamar la atención
sobre las horas en que transmitiremos.
Forman parte de este número los correspondientes al mes de abril.

dunia, buni-bunian bagus dan pidatou hal kebudayaan Nippon dikirimkan ke mana 2, selama 41 jam lamanya den-

SHORT-WAVE BROADCAST FROM TOKYO
HE Broadcasting Corporation of Japan sends out short-wave transmissions as indicated by the
following chart.
Transmissions are pointed to 13 parts of the globe, the total transmission time amounting
to 41 hours a day in 16 languages. This means virtually that Japan's short-wave broadcasts cover
the entire world.
Special attention of the listeners is called to the fact that occasional changes in frequencies
and other technical details may be made inevitably upon the change of seasons. Please note
this bulletin each month, never taking it for granted.
Reports on your reception of, and reaction to, our programmes are solicited most eagerly.
The communications should be addressed to the
Foreign Department
Identification Signal

Dear Mr. Hamilton:
Enclosed is a special report dealing with developments
on the Japanese radio which seemed to foreshadow a move
toward Southeastern Asia.
894.76/50
Unfortunately, until our reorganization plan is approved,
we are unable to study the Japanese material until several
days after it has been transmitted. With the speed-up
that we are now planning, we would have been in a position
to give you this information some days ago.
894.76
At this time we are issuing Spot Bulletins and Special
Reports despite the fact that the Foreign Broaccast Moni-
toring Service is still in its preliminary stages, so that
it is as yet unable to issue the projected series of regular
daily reports.
This is being sent to you in accordance with the plans for
routing material worked out in a recent conference between
representatives of the State Department and of the Foreign
Broadcast Monitoring Service.
SEP
Sincerely yours,

ay

=== Page 166 === (confidence 0.99)
FEDERAL COLMUNICATIONS COLMISSION
FOREIGN BROADCAST MONITORING SERVICE
cial Report #2
July 22, 1941
TOKYO BROADCASTS TO NORTH AJERICA
June 23 to July 18
Intense preoccupation with Southeastern Asia dating back to the
outbreak of the Soviet-German War is evident in Tokyo radio from June
23 to July 18. The other disting ishing characteristic of broadcasts
during this period is an apparent weakening of Axis ties.
These two trends appear simltaneously and are reinforced by
the increased emphasis in radio commentary on Japan's independent
national policy. Reconstructior of Konoye's Cabinet on July 18 only
serves to underline the Tokyo ralio picture of Japanese policy as
becoming increasingly self-cons:ious, diciplined, and totalitarian
but less Axis-oriented.
The basis for these inferences includes:
a. Mounting hostiliy toward Britain and the United States,
including belligerent rea tion to British "military preparations
in southeast Asia."
b. Protest against "Third Pover encirclement" of Japan in
rumors of Anglo-Chungkin, alliance.
c. Atrocity storie: and reports of anti-Japanese activities
in Indo-China.
d. Studied neutraliy vis a vis the Soviet-German ar.
The pattern of treatment f Southeastern Asia shows remarkable
similarity to that which has p eviously preceded the Japanese moves
in that direction.
SOUTHEASTERN ASIA
"American Provocations:"
The stage for such a new orientation was set by July lst when
reports of increased American ir aid to Chungking were played up
with lengthy quotations from te Tokyo Nichi Nichi:
"It would be fair t, assume," the paper asserts, "that the
United States does not vish to fight Japan, to fight in the Orient

any more than Japan. If this is so, the reported American aid to
Chungking certainly does not improve the matter in any way. It
provides no material threat to Japan but it succeeds in offending
the Japanese Government. If there is anything that Japan would
not tolerate, it is the spread of the European conflict to East
Asia and undermining activities of the Third Powers against the
proposed establishment of a friendly sphere in this part of the
world."
The line is continued on July 2 in a report attributed to
Shanghai:
"The United States still sticks to the anti-Japanese
policy, extending its aid to Chungking in the field of finance,
aviation and other war materials and further dispatching military
advisers to consolidate defenses at such bases as Singapore,
Manila and others in co-operation with the British. Not content
with this, America's instigating hands have been extended to
the Netherlands East Indies against Japan in the Far East."
On July 5th, again commenting on arrival of arms in Burma, is the following:
"Officials in Tokyo pointed out that the Japanese Govern-
ment is not sitting idly over-looking such hostile acts under
its very nose."
"Third Power Encirclement:"
On July 2nd, and repeated on July 13th, are reports "from Bangkok"
of increased British military and air concentrations on the Burmese
and Jandalay frontier of Thailand. On July 12th the following report
"from Nanking" was heard:
"British maneuvers heve culminated in the conclusion of a
military alliance with the Chungking regime in Burma and the
Malayan Peninsua. The alliance were also reported to stipulate
the establishment of a general staff of the Allied Army under
Air Marshell Brooke-Popham, Commander-in-Chief of the British
forces."
The importance attached to this rumor is shown by extensive press
opinion quoted on the same day, and by the following comments:
"Close attention is now being paid by various circles to
the threatening attitude on the part of Britain when there is no
apparent reason for it."
Indo-China:
Supplementing this line are sporadic reports which are sugges-
tive of present and future Japanese diplomatic pressure in Indo-China.

nclude alleged mistreatment of pro-Japanese Annamite elements and

REACTIONS TO SOVIET-GERIAN WAR
In contrast to a feigned or real belligerence in the southern
sector, the radio exhibits a comparative indifference in its treat-
ment of the Soviet-German War and a remarkable lack of concern for
the Siberian area. While news reports of hostilities in Europe are
largely of German origin, there is no specific anti-Soviet comment,
nor is there any reaffirmation of Axis ties. American aid to the
Soviet is given cursory disapproval as being contrary to expressed
American rinciples, but accent on blockade is lacking, as is news
and comment on reported Soviet mining operations off Vladivostock.
Neither is mention made of Japanese troop movements to the North.
On the contrary, an interview of the Japanese iinister in Woscow
with Foreign Commissar Molotov is reported as follows on July 16,
and again on July 18:
"During this interview the Soviet Foreign Commissar is
understood to have explained that the newly concluded Anglo-
Soviet military agreement applies only to the present conflict
with Germany."
An attitude of suspended action is definitely indicated in the
Tokyo radio's treatment of Russia.

Comments on Current Events:
To talk on the publication or the Kokumin-No-Sho
(Nation's Text-Book)": This text-book is made up of three chapt-
ers: The first chapter explains the creation of a new world or-
der, the second deals with the international situation and the
course to be taken by the nation and the third stresses the put-
ting into practice of the course to be taken by the nation. The
first chapter begins with the middle ages and explains the im-
perialistic trend of the various nations and how it was stimulated
all the more by the World Var, which strengthened the supremacy
of England, America and France. It explains how Japan in Asia
and Germany in Europe has risen to break this supremacy. In other
words, to save the small nations of the orld, which hed become
the colonial settlements of England, America and France through
their sinister schemes, Japan has risen in East Asia to create
a new order, followed by Germany, who is attemoting to reconstruct
herself and create a new order in Europe, thus bringing about
the second European war. Thus, the first chapter explains the
developments up till the outbreak of the second European war.
The attitude of why the old order must be liquidated is, in other
words, the tradition of tradition of creation of a new order, and
the chapter on the course of tho nation fully stresses the signi-
ficance of the creation of this new order and deeply anal zing
this from various angles, it explains the attitude to be taken
by the nation, as follovs: "To liquidate the oppression of the
American and European nations, such as England and America, wno
had been maintaining their prosperity at the expense of the small-
er nations, Japan rose up in 1932 to save Manchuria and urged her
to become independent. After establishing lanchuria as an indepen-
dent nation, she has beeh strivin; to create lasting peace in
East Asia." In otherwords, the transition of the present vorld
trend, namely, from tho old order into the nev order, is regerded
as having started from the independence of anchurie. With the
independence of Manchuria as the start, this new trend has expand-
ed into the Near East and the nations of the south, such as Turkey,
Arabia, Thailand, Burma and India, which had been under the oppres-
sion of the American and European nations. However, due to the
long years of tradition, nany of the smaller nations thouit it
only proper to remain under the control of the American and Eu-
ropean nations. A typical example of this is China. In other
words, Japan has risen to cnlighten China, strengthen the colla-
boration between Japan and China, create a now sphere of Mutual

=== Page 177 === (confidence 0.98)
ge #2
Prosperity and existence and create a new order in East Asia,
besides contributing toward the establishment of world peace.
Since world peace cannot be expected to created at once, the
creation of a new order in East Asia can be said to be the first
step toward the establishment of world peace. Consequently, Japan
will fight to the end until collaboration with a new China is es-
tablished. This has been repeatedly stated end is Japan's fixed
policy. This is strongly stressed in the Nation's Text-Book.
Thus, the creation of a strong national defense is necessary. In
other words, it means the creation of a national solidarity in
which economic and diplomatic moves are made to act in concert
with the armed force to accomplish our country's policy. The
creation of this national solidarity of Japan, as leader of East
Asia and a powerful nation to bring about worid peace, is most
important end shculd be accomplished as early as possible. Thus,
the Nation's Text-Book stresses that the people should serve their
country with the "Nation First" idea in order to create this na-
tional solidarity.

Conment on Current Events:
To talk on the coalition of England, America,
Russia and Chungking: A month has pa: sed since the outbreak
of wer between Germany end Russia. During this time, fierce
fighting has been staged between German and Russian troops along
the whole front stretching fonn the Finnish border on the north
to the Black Sea on the South. On reviewing the developments of
the war, it seems that the German troops have maintained a su-
perior position right along and the defeat of the enemy has be-
come obvious. After only a month of the war, considerable truth
can be seen in Germeny's announcement that the greater part of
the Russian air force has been destroyed, and the same can be
said of the success of the German mechanized troops.

=== Page 180 === (confidence 0.99)
#3
Thus, the German troops have captured Smolensk and are now but
120 kilometers away from the Stalin line. Besides, in the north,
German troops are advancing toward Leningrad from the north and
south. Even in the south, downfall of Kiev has become imminent.
(Mentioned Stalin's assumption of the post of Minister of War
as an indication that the war has reached its climax; and as a
step to prepare against internal revolution.) Together with
Soviet Russia, who is worrying about her downfall, are America
and England and Chungking, which is continuing its resistance
with the aid of England and America, and also the Dutch East
Indies, which is trying to preserve her existence through coali-
tion with England and America. For what particular reason are
they shivering with fear to such an extent? The reasons are as
follow: Chungking is fearing that Japan might undertake a great
push into the interior with the defeat of Soviet Russia becoming
imminent and ceuse her complete downfall. As for England,she is
fearing that Japan might threaten her possessions and interests
in East Asia. As for Dutch East Indies, she voluntarily refused
to become a part of the greater East Asia Mutual Prosperity Sphere
and due to this stubbornness and unwise attitude of hers; she is
fearing each and every move that Japan might make. Thus, she is
simultaneously trying to ally herself firmly with England and
America. On the other hand, England and Amerida are desperately
to preserve their interests in East Asia through their aid to
Chiang Kai-shek. Furthermore, they are stretching their sinister
hands toward Theiland, not to mention the Dutch East Indies, and
are striving to make Thailand anti-Japanese and take on the brunt
of their anti-Japanese blockade. That such collaboration among
England and America, and also Chungking has become all the more
open with the devolopment of the Russo-German war is appearing
in various forms and shapes must be said to be the greetest con-
cern of Japan. Those that have already been reported in news
are the Chungking's troops' preparation to march into French

by Zondo Naomi, of Chugai Shogyo (Domostic & Forcign Commercial News):

=== Page 195 === (confidence 0.99)
Page7
your country. Primarily speaking, this action of England and America was
taken in resentment against the joint defense agreement reached between
Japan and France for the protection of French Indo-China and certainly was

is an irrational declaration advocating the disarmament of all na-

domination of thc world by these nations, which are menacing world
civilization. The intention of America, who is planning to send

war into the area where peace is being maintained by Japan alone
and Japan is fully aware of this. (Record indistinct)".

Comment on current events:
Topic "England and America's aid toward Soviet Russia":
Immediately after the announcoment of the joint declaration by
President Roosevclt and Premier Churchill on August 14, the British
Government, as though she were waiting for it, invoked a total em-
bargo against Japan and announced her loan of 10,O00 pounds to Sovict
Russia. Furthermore, according to a reliable report, plans for the
holding of a thrce-nation conference at Moscow sometime in September
have been made and Chiang Kai-shek, too, probably will take part in
this conference to discuss the strengthening of the encirclement
against Japan. Judging from the above-mentioned reports, we can
clearly perceive that the aim of England and America hidden behind
their joint declaration is their ambition to dominate the world. In
other words, the conference of the two leaders of England and America
on the Atlantic was nothing other than a discussion in regard to
their joint action in Europe, Asia, South America as well as Africa
after the conclusion of the war. And one of the important matters
discussed was their aid to Soviet Russia, which I am now attempting
to explain. There is no room for doubt that the concrete plans in
regard to their aid to Soviet Russia will be discussed with Stalin
at the proposed Moscow conferencc. This is a thing of great

=== Page 227 === (confidence 0.99)
age #3
importance. The reason is that the real aim of England snd America
is not to help Soviet Russia but to exhaust Germany's strength at
the expense of Soviet Russia. At the same time, their aim is nothing
other than to instigate (?) Soviet Russia's Far Eastern Air Force
and utilize it in the encirclement of Japan. Generally speaking,
President Roosevelt's primary aim is to aid England. Hence, America's
aid to Sovict Russia is only of & secondary nature. Taking advan-
tage of this golden opportunity brought about by the outbreak of
war between Germany and Soviet Russia, America is trying to strengthen
England's fighting power. Although Soviet Russia is putting up a
great resistance against Germany and is preventing Germany from re-
alizing a quick decisive victory, it is an obvious fact that Soviet
Russia, sooner or later, will be defeated in view of her great
losses, both in materials and men. Thus America, whose primary aim
is to aid England, is now being confronted wi th the predicament of
considering aid to Soviet Russia seriously. Be it Premier Churchill,
who is anti-Russian, or President Roosevelt, who is anti-Japanese,
if their real feelings wero to be revealed, it can be said that they
are realing hoping for Sovict Russia and Germany to cut each other's
throat. Since it is believed that the aim of Premier Churchill and
President Roosevclt is to utilize Soviet Russia and bring about the
downfall of Hitler, it is believed that England and America's aid to
Soviet Russia will pivot around this principle. (Record indistinct).
Hence, England and America have come to realize the necessity for
the conclusion of a tripartite military alliance and it is the con-
census of opinion that what Soviet Russia most urgently needs are
airplanes, for it is being reported that Soviet Russia is now using
a large number of planes from her Far Eastern Air Force, which sho
had hoped to keep out of the war, to augment the heavy losses suf-
fered since the beginning of the war. This is the reason why America
is increasing her aid, in spite of the fact that her production and
transportation facilities are not sufficient. Already, numerous
American ships carrying aviation gasoline as well as other war mater-
ials reportedly have left America for Vladivostok. Furthermore,
large numbers of heavy bombers have already been flown along the
Great Circle route to Soviet Russia and American military experts
have stated that even fast fighting planes can be flown to Soviet
Russia by way of Burbanks, Dutch Harbor, Aleutian Islands and Kam-
chatka. Thus, when the statoment of Secrotary of War Stimson that
the defense of the Aleutians and Alaska has become of great impor-
tance is considered togethcr with the above reports, its significance
becomes very clear. (Record indistinct). Whatever it may be in
form, it matters little, since American ships have already left for
Vladivostok and planes are being sent to Soviet Russia by way of
Kamchatka and other Sovict territories. (Record indistinct). In
other words, America is approaching the realization of her problem
of threatening our country from the north. When it is thus considered
we find that England and America's aid to Soviet Russia will have a
great effect upon Japan. Furthermore, there is an opinion that
England and America will make somo sort of request to Soviet Russia

=== Page 228 === (confidence 0.99)
Page #4
in regard to the encirclement of Japan at the Moscow conference in
return for their aid. Instead of being merely an opinion, it is a
thing which is possible. In choosing Vladivostok, which is close
to Japan, as the port for the unloading of the materials of aid to
Soviet Russia, America has outrageously ignored Japan's position.
This must be regarded as her attempt to aggravate intentionally the
already acute situation between Japan and America more than neces-
sary. This joint action of England and America who are threatening
Japan from the north must be regarded seriously by Japan, in view
of their ABCD bloc on the south. In connection with this, we would
like to mention that a strong opinion to the effect that our country
should watch seriously the insolence of England, America and Soviet
Russia in holding the threc-nation confcrence and should bo prepared
with an appropriate counter-measure is bcing voiced in some circles.
No matter what action England and America may take, Japan's funda-
mental policy in East Asia will not be shaken, So long as England
and America do not amend their scheme against Japan, Japan and
England and America will move along a parallel line and if England
and America should continue to hamper Japan's progress, Japan in-
evitably will have to overcome this obstacle with her strength.
Domestic nows (Kanto District):
Nothin f particular interest.
Talk on the international situation by Wakao Oiwa of Domei
News Agency: Thc talk primarily centercd on the progress of the
Nanking government and explained this progress as attributable to
America's freezing of Chinese assets. In other words, America's
freezing of Chinese assets executed on the request of the Chungking
government has compelled the Nanking government to devise measures
contributing toward her self-sufficiency. The speaker also commented
upon the joint declaration of Prosident Roosevelt and Premior
Churchill and explained its aim as aid to Soviet Russia and to
Chungking. In regard to the aid to the Chungking government, the
speaker stated that it would be of no use due to the rigid blockade
of the China coast. Furthermore, the speaker stated that America
now is in no position to consider civing positive aid to Chungking
in view of the fact that aid to Russia has become of greater noces-
sity, in order to accomplish her aim of prolonging the European
war and exhausting Germany's strength. In closing, the speaker
stated that Japan is preparcd to protcct the independence of the
races of East Asia and fight for the peace and prosperity of these
races and urged them to unite as one to protect themselves. (Rccord
not very cloar so the talk has been digosted.)

A story told by a professional story-teller.
News:
Representatives of England, America and Russia re-
portedly will meet in Moscow sometime in the early part of September.
Chiang Kai-shek reportedly will also take part in the discussion.
Howuvor, America's hostile action toward Japan has increascd of
late. American ships reportedly are already heading toward Vladi-
vostok with war materials, while American-made planes are being
flown to Russia by way of Alaska, necessitating the strengthening
of America's Aluskan bases. Hence, Japan is now being threatened
from the north as well as the south. Some quarters in Japan are
urging the government to take sterner action against America.
Newspapers of Japan today editorialized on England::
and America's proposal to hold a conference with Russia at Moscow.
Some of tho newspapers have even stated that Japan will take armed
action against England and America if they insist on hampering
Japan's move. Others, like the Yomiuri Shimbun and Hochi Shimbun,
stated that Japan cannot remain unconcerned about the Moscow con-
ference and that the shipment of aviation gasoline and other materi-
als to Vladivostok is a direct disregard of Japan's position in the
Eastern Pacific and cannot be ignored.
According to a despatch from Rome, Editor Gayda of
Giornale d'Italia wrote an editorial entitled "Japan's Role" on
August 17 and warned Amcrica as follows: "Before rushing headlong
into adventure, Presidont Roosevelt should first consider Japan's
strength. As it can bo clcarly seen by the fact that England, with
whom America is maintaining a closo collaboration, is greatly con-
cerned about the armed existcnce of Japan, Japan is a great power
not to be ignored by America. In spite of the fact that she has
been fighting against Chiang Kai-shek for the past four years, Japan
is showing no sign of having weakened. Instead, it can be said that
her strength has increased. Her naval air force, especially, has
not even lost its strength, but is controlling a wide region, rich
in natural resources and strategic points. If it had not been for
Japan's existence, England would have lessened the strength of her
Far Eastern navy and also would have used Australian and Indian
troops on a large scale in Europe. In this respect, America should
seriously consider the great strength possessed by Japan." Thus
did Editor Gayda point out the important position of Japan in the
Pacific.
The Cabinet today adopted the suggestion made by
Shozo Murata, Minister of Communications and Railways, for the govern-
ment control of shipping.

Japanese naval air force, divided into three groups,
today raided the various important military bases in Szechwan Pro-
vince.
Condition of Chungking becoming worse with America
meddling into her internal affairs. Dissatisfaction against America,
thus, is gradually becoming great.
According to a report reaching Canton from a reliable
source in Hongkong, Shin Sai-do, an important official of Chungking,
is making no attempt to return to Chungking ever since his arrival
in Hongkong. Described the pitiful condition of Chungking as fol-
lows: "Shortage of food-stuffs in Chungking is very great, and the
soldiers as well as the people are unable to obtain rice. Due to
the shortage of food-stuffs and the rise of prices, real meal is
eaten even among officials only twice a week. Besides, due to the
repeated raids of the Japanesc air force, the majority of thc people
are spending thoir days in the bombproof shelters. Unable to stand
the inconveniences any longer, I have escaped from Chungking on the
pretext of visiting my sick friend and I have no intention of re-
turning to Chungking again."
According to a despatch from Shanghai, American and
British investments in Shanghai are being sold. Investments probably
to be turned toward India and South America.
According to a despatch from Hongkong, the city is
about to oxperience a great shortage of food-stuffs due to Japan's
freezing act. Due to this condition, anti-British sentiment re-
portedly is increasing.
According to a despatch from Shanghai, the Americans
in Shanghai are suffering more than the Japanese from America's
freezing act. There reportedly are 394 American shops and firms
whose customers primarily are Japanese and Chinese subjects of the
Nanking government. Consequently, they are doing their utmost to
cooperate with the Japanese.

News:
The raising of the standard of the Thai Legation to
that of an Embassy was announced today in the official gazette.
According to a despatch from San Francisco, Consul-
General Muto, who was called to Washington by Ambassador Nomura,
returned to San Francisco on August 15 with a message of encourage-
ment from Ambassador Nomura to the Japanese residents along the
Pacific Coast. The message read as follows: "At a time when the
relations between Japan and America are very accute, I am happy to
see the Japanese residents so calmly coping with the situation.
I, too, will do my best to reestablish the Japanese-American re-
lations and will work toward the accomplishment of my aim to the
last. I sincerely hope that you, Japanese residents, will keep cool
to the very end and strive diligently to cope with the situation.
According to a despatch from New York, England has
started an intensive propaganda in regard to the President Roosevelt-
Premier Churchill conference. She reportedly broadcast the eight
points of the joint declaration in forty languages and is preparing
leaflets to spread over the territories occupied by Germany. However,
the British people are very dissatisfied with this conference, ac-
cording to a news broadcaster of Columbia Broadcasting Station sta-
tioned in London. On the other hand, America, too, reportedly has
started to propagandize this conference, but some senators are burn-
ing with indignation at the discrimination shown against American
newspapers (while two British newspapermen and cameramen were allowed
to be present, none were allowed from the American newspapers) and
many aro regarding this as an attompt to keep from the people the
fact that America has been duped by England and criticizing the con-
ference as a repetition of Wilson's blunder. Furthermore, the as-
surance of aid to Soviet Russia and the assumption of joint action
with Stalin has caused the indignation of the American churches. and
the American Church Federation has started an opposition movement
claiming that to aid Moscow, which does not recognize religion and
private property and is scheming for the bolshevization of the world,
will only make the Communists in America puff up and endanger the
Americans. In view of this difference of opinion, a disturbance will
inevitable break out sooner or later.
According to a despatch from New York, it is regarded
that America's airplane production power will not increasc. Even
since President Roosevelt announced that 500 air fortresses will be
constructed every month until the end of next year, two months have

=== Page 234 === (confidence 0.99)
age #2
passed but no progress has been made. Consequently, it is being re-
garded that the production of the required number of plancs by the
end of next year will be impossible. Due to the shortage of acces-
sories and due to strikes, only 20 planes actually are being pro-
duccd in one month.
According to a communique issued by the German High
Command on August 17, the allied troops of Germany and Hungary have
finally captured Nicholaev, which is the second largest ship-building
center of Soviet Russia. Furthermore, according to a DNB despatch,
the German air force raided Russian communication, railroad and
road facilities on August 16 and 17 and caused considerable confusion
among the Russian troops along the Dnieper River at the central front
and Odessa's fall is now a matter of time.
According to a despatch from a reliable source, pre-
parations are being made to establish an independent government in
Ukrainia.
According to a despatch from Germany, since 70% of
the strength of Russia has alroady been destroyed, Germany expects
to finish her schedule of the war by the end of September at the
present rate of progress.

Comment on current events:
Topic "Japan's war preparation": The foundation of
the greater East Asia mutual prosperity sphere has been made un-
shakable through the conclusion of the joint defense agreement of
French Indo-China, but with tho rapprochement of French Indo-China
toward Japan, the political, economic and military coercion of the
third powers is becomin stronger day by day. Around Japan, China
and Manchoukuo and including French Indo-China, the so-called ABCD
encirclement has been formed, and unreasonable, hostile measures
are being enacted more and more every day. By way of an illustra-
tion,if Japan were to be rogardod as a great rock standing upright
in the middle of a wide sea, it can be described as a succession of

=== Page 272 === (confidence 0.99)
Page #4
great waves striking against the rock and trying to knock it down.
And the situation in East Asia can be said to be like the grcat rock,
Japan, with its foundations buried deep and wide, withstanding tho
repeated beatings of the great waves. Nevertheless, the ever-
changing objective situation of the world--namely, the dashing waves-
appears to have the possibility of becoming greater with the passing
of the days. In view of such a state of affairs, Japan's mission of
creating a greater East Asia mutual prospority sphere has come to.
hold a greater significance and is being pressed to make a rapid
progress. In proportion to this, Japan, who must go through with
this important mission, is now being pressed with the nocessity of
cstablishing a fimn, unshakable footing. Hence, recent national
movements have all beon furthered along this line and the so-called
war preparations footing has been taken for the express purpese of
perfecting our national defense. Consequently, our fcllow-countrios,
full of peaceful intentions and with a strong determination to pro-
tect our country's rights, have united as one and are striving to
establish a coordinated footing. As a result, an unshakable national
footing has been brought about. Now, let us pick out and report to
you roughly the very recent movements working for the perfection of
our war preparation footing. What is first worthy of note is the
16th and 17th meeting of the national mobilization committee hold
from the latter part of last month to the beginning of this month.
At this meeting of the national mobilization committec, the important
industrial organizations control bill, the coastal trade control
bill, and the amendments to the ocean transportation control law,
the bill pertaining to the withdrawal of gold and other important
minerals and eight other important matters to bo enacted as imperial
decrees were adopted. These bills have all been drafted and are
expected to be promulgated and enforced sometime this month. All
of thesc bills are aimed for the strengthening of the mobilization
law and are expected to expand production, harmonize transportation,
and expedito distribution of manufactured goods, and to be able to
cope with any situation with this as a basis. In conjunction with
this, measures to prevent malignant inflntion have also been adopted.
(Record indistinct). Of those, the important trade control bill and
the industrial organizations control bill have been commented upon
in a previous comment on current cvents and thus no further mention
will be made. So, today, let us comment in a somewhat detailed
manner upon the amendments to the ocean transportation control law.
In a word, these anendments will place ocean transportation under
the management of the government and through it our country's ocean
transportation will be brought up to the highest level. Its aim can
be stated as follows: Firstly, to enable the government to comuan-
deer all vosscls in time of war and to utilize inevitably these ves-
sels according to the transportation plan and war operation (?) plan
established by the government. Secondly, to enable the government
to commandeer the services of the ships' crews simultaneously with
the commandeering of the vesscls and make them sorve in accordance

=== Page 273 === (confidence 0.99)
Pagc # 5
with the allotment (?) and wages established by the government.
Thirdly, to cnable the government to take over the management of
the shipyards and factories manufacturing engines and accessories.
These are the three aims, but through them the government will be
able to exercise control over the three important branches of ves-
sels, crews and ship-building. Hence, the war preparation of the
shipping trade of Japan, which is surrounded by water on all sides,
has been given a firm foundation. Next, I would like to talk
about the transportation service (?). In regard to this, Minister
of Finance Ogura expressed a very constructive view before the
national finance discussion committee, which was held on August 20.
The gist of the Finance Minister's talk pertained to the determina-
tion of the financial circle to cope with the present acute internal
and external situation and the government:s attitude and urged the
expansion of production under the war preparation footing and the
harmonization of the circulation of money for the purpose of in-
creasing the industrial strength. Furthermore, on the following
day, August 21, 11 banks, mostly industrial, such as the Mitsui,
Mitsubishi, Yasuda and Sumitomo, formed a credit merger union (in
other words, an organization for the cooperation of mutual credit)
to help in the development of industries in this emergency. Now
let us consider the reaction of the nation toward the activities
of the government. In regard to this, the nation, too, as indicated
by the above-mentioned constructive movement of the financial circle,
is unitedly cooperating with the government. In other words, the
attitude of the nation to realize fully the mission assigned to it
by the government and to strive unitediy for the enhancement of our
national power without any sign of haughtiness is very reassuring.
Thus the nation today is burning with the spirit to serve their
country and is earnestly striving to put into practice the way of
the Gods in their respective occupations. However, in order to
strengthen further this spirit, national service societies have been
formed throughout the country with the National Support Socicty as
their leader for the purposo of carrying out a strong national ser-
vice movement. The aim of this is to wipe out liberalism and egoism
and to establish a nationwide labor service footing in conformity
with the aim of our country. In oter words, the primary aim is to
make the young as well as the old and the men as well as the women
all work for our country. Other things that can bec considered in
conjunction with this are the organization of the national support
youths' and childrens' organizations and the birth of the students'
national service societies, composed primarily of students of uni-
versities and technical colleges. In fact, such things as the
nationwide control of youths' organizations, with their important
task of increasing production, the national service movement of the
students, upon whom rest the fate of our country, and the merging
of the women's organizations can be said to be an indication of tho
fact that everyone residing within Japan is working for the enhance-
ment of our country. Besides, in order to solidify this national

=== Page 274 === (confidence 0.99)
Page #6
unity and guide it most effectively, the national support society
recently compiled standardized booklets explaining the practical
theory of the national support society and distributed them through-
out the country. In a word, these booklets explained in simple
words the fundamental idea of the national service movement and
the course to be taken by the nation in this emergency and sought
for the determination and resolution of the nation to strive for
the creation of a new order in East Asia, the establishment of world
peace in the long run. Not only in East Asia, but in every part of
the world, the people are experiencing the pains accompanying the
birth of a new world. Japan in East Asia and Germany and Italy in
Europe can safely be said to be the three greatest nations of the
new world. Under this great mission, our country Japan has been
fighting for the past four years. Nevertheless, the situation in

News:
That a discussion between Japan and America has been
started in regard to the Pacific probles is as exactly as was
announced by our information bureau on August 29. In regard to
the attitude of the nation against this, they are maintaining an
extremely clam and unperturbed attitude. Originally speaking,
Japan and America are not opposing each other of their own accord,
but has been compelled to do so because of their conflicting in-
terests in East Asia. In considering this delicate situation, what
should be of special interest is evil scheme of England, who is
trying to better her position by bringing about a clash between
Jepan and America, and the other powers, Consequently, in carry-
ing on the negotiation with Anerica, our country should guard
against the defanation and false propaganda or the hostile third
powers and against taking any hasty action. It is only proper for
Japan, who loves world peace, to nake every peaceful effort to
prevent any unnecessary friction motivated by the evil scheme and
misunderstandinf of the third powers and to devise diplonatic
lieasures for the restoration of a nornal condition between Japan
and America. However, in as much as our diplomatic policy, as
was explained in the imperial decree at the time of the conclusion
of the tripartite alliance, pivots on the tripartite alliance and
our fundariental policy for the establishment of an East Asia u-
tual prosperity sphere and the acconplishment of the China Inci-
dent is unchangeable, it is needless to mention that anything that
runs counter to the spirit of the tripartite alliance and that
changes, even slightly, the aim of the establishrent of a mutual
prosperity sphere and the settle:ent cf the China Incident is not
permissible. Hence, it is believed that diplonatic measures that
will make this spirit more effective will be taken. Through the
exchange of nessages between President Roosevelt end Premier Ko-
moye, our government has finally brought to light the diplcmatic
negotiation between Jepan and Aierica and will probably take a
more concrete Measure with the return of Minister Wakatsuki, who
has resided in America for 15 years. Since tho success of this
negotiation is an extremely inportant one, the nation is calmly
vatching its developments.

rice production of places in Holkaido, Aomori Prefecture and Iwate

Japan districts, as a vhole, will be greater than last year to
make up for this shortage. This is wholly attributable to the
patriotic devotion of the farrers to the Inperor and this patrio-
tic spirit is not only being seen in the agrarian districts, but
everywhere, even in the mining districts and at home. Hence, the
way of the Gods (patrictic spirit) is being practised in cvery
line of work. It if were not so, it vould be impossible for
Japan to establish a strong national defense. Consequently, the
National Support Society, having set aside the first day of every
month as National Service Day, is assisting in tho rationaliza-
tion of the national living to prevent any waste. For oxample,
the aim of the national service day of July 1 was the unification
of national living, while the ain of the national service day of
August 1 was to establish correct living. In other words, the
Hational Support Society is striving to independent manner of
living of each individual into one of group or mass living--that
is, a life of all combining their strength and assisting each
other. For example, a cornunity kitchcn was established in a cer-
tain village of Chiba Prefecture and all the villagers were fed

Ampliación de Nuestra Trasmisión
Se ha estado llevando a cabo desde el comienzo del presente año una gran ampliación de nuestra
trasmisión a ultramar, incluso la transmisión a Sudamérica.
Estamos trasmitiendo a trece direcciones durante cuarenta y una horas al dia, empleando diecisei
idiomas inclusive el español y el portugués.
Lamentamos no disponer todavia de los detalles del programa y nos permitimos llamar la atención
sobre las horas en que transmitiremos.
Forman parte de este número los correspondientes al mes de abril.

Comment on current events:
Topic "The new order in Europe and America's
recent activities": (Condensed) It is being claimed that
the war, today, is being fought with two views, the construc-
tive and the destructive. The war to create a new order is
the constructive war and the war to break down this new or-
der, which is being created, is the destructive war. The
old view that war is destructive has been toppled from its
foundation by Japan's constructive effort in the current
China incident, and Germany's creation of a new order in
Europe has substantiated this. The question as to whether
the constructive or the destructive force will win is now
a matter of the past, because the world must now a dmit
that a new order is being created in two parts of the world.
The two places are East Asia and Europe. With the decline
of the fighting power of Chungking in East Asia, Japan is
gradually creating a new China. This noble project has
come to affect the various parts of East Asia, including
French IndoChina and Thailand. On the other hand, although
it appears as if tho Russo-German war is delaying the cze-
ation of a new order in Europe, it is merely an illusion
caused by the protraction of the war in contrast to Germeny's
quick victories in the previous campaigns. This idea that
a war between two great nations of the world should be
settled in two or three months must be termed a sross lack
of common sense. However, judging from the progress nade
by the Gorman troops, it can be readily imagined that the
first stage of tho war will be concluded in a comparatively
short time. This is because the German authorities have
expressed the confidence that groat progross will be made
by tho coming of Winter and that Germany will not repeat
tho failuro of Napoloon. Today, it is boing gonerally ad-
mitted that Gormany had contemplated the Russo-German war.
It is true, but Gormany did not contemplato war against
Russia because Russia would hamper the c reation of a new
order in Europe, but because it was necessary to chango
the condition in Soviet Russia in order to creato a now
order in Europe. Congequently, the slow but steady progress
of tho Gorman troops will live on as the mine creating the
now ordor. In this manner, constructive offort is being
exerted in the East and ths West. In regard to tho China

1941
Radio Tokyo continues to treat the subject of the KurusuDepartment of State
mission to Washington in a desultory manner. Evidences of
"backing-down" or of "moderation" regarding the United States
are less apparent on the shortwave than they are in the Japanese
press. Great emphasis is placed on the "gravity" of the Kibi Maru
sinking, and on the "fabricated so-called Southeast Asia crisis."
Tokyo seems bent on building up grievances both to the South and
the North. Koh Ishii's facetious reference to freedom of the seas
894.76/62
is used as part of Tokyo's studied "eat-your-words" theme, which
894.76
is apparently carried on for possible future use in the event of
new Japanese military thrusts when American hypocrisy would again
have to be stressed.
Tokyo radio gives the impression that the domestic press is
treating the Kurusu mission in a discrepant, almost perplexed fashiong
P
All the newspapers apparently agree with NICHI NICHI in the state-
ment, "The limit to which Japan can go is definitely decided," but
while NICHI NICHI nonetheless urges the people to "prepare for the
worst," ASAHI argues that if the United States has "even a litt
6
0V 14 1941
respect for Japan's stand, a peaceful settlement will not be
FILED
impossible."
Tokyo is becoming more and more incautious in its editorial
treatment of questions relating to the Soviet Union. Detailed
reparts

unemotional and objective treatment of the September sinking of
a Japanese vessel by a floating Russian mine.
... Other evidences of the same trend are seen in a unique

While Tokyo radio almost frantically denies the "fabricated

Japanese drive into Siberia.

News:
Achievements of the Japanese troops in Central China
during the past two years-- Fought against 1,800,000 enemy troops,

According to a dispatch from Hankem, our troops, occu-
pying Jisho in Hupeh Province, repulsed the counter attack of the
Chinese troops the day before yesterday and killed about 1,200 enemy
troops. In spite of this, Chungking issued a false propaganda claim-
ing the recapture of Jisho.
Achievement of the Japanese troops operating in Chekiang
during the past week -- killed 3,037 enemy troops, captured 433 and
many arms and ammunitions.
Prince Takamatsu yesterday was elected as president of
