News:
According to a dispatch from New York, the German troops
are making steady progress on all sides toward Moscow. On October!,
they reportedly captured Tula and advanced to a point 14o kilometers
south of Moscow. In view of the danger confronting Moscow, the
Russian government on October 1l reportedly issued an order for the
evacuation of all women and children from Moscow. On the other hand,
the German troops fighting in the south sector reportedly wiped out
two Russian divisions with the aid of the German air force and have
captured Taganrog. In regard to this, the German High Command on
October 11 issued a special communique claiming the conclusion of the
fight in the south. The communique also stated that the German troops
in the south sector have captured 106,300 Russian troops, 212 armored
trucks and tanks and 672 guns since September 26.
In accordance with the order issued by Eirikko, commander-
in-chief of the first defense area, the Chinese third division
reportedly has taken up defense positions in the area southwest of
Teishu and are attempting to recapture Telshu and the neighboring
regions. However, the achievements of the Japanese troops in Hunan,
as of October 11, include 2,600 Chinese troops killed, 300 captured,
besides the capture of 350 rifles and many arms and ammunition.
Similar progress is being made by tho Japanese troops in Shangtung
Province.
Yesterday, October 1l, was the day the Japanese troops
made a landing at Bias Bay three years ago and started their drive
toward Canton. And it was today, three years ago, that Canton was
captured, thus cutting off the supply line of Chungking from the
south.
After negotiating with the American government, the Japan-
ese government yesterday announced the resumption of passenger service
between Japan and America from October 15. It was announced that the
Tatsuta Maru, Nitta Maru and Taiyo Maru will be dispatched to America.
Of these three ships, the Tatsuta Maru will leave on October 15 for
Hawaii and then sail to San Francisco. At the same time, the Japan-
ese government announced that the dispatching of these ships does not
mean that Japan plans to evacuate the Japanese residing in America.
To make a survey of the financial condition in the various
parts of Manchoukuo, North China, Mongolia and Central China, .....
left Tokyo this morning. After visiting Ise Shrine on the way, he
will leave for his destination from Kobe on October 14.
To promote friendly relations with the students from Man-
choukuo and China in Japan, the East Asia Students' Federation today
-1-

=== Page 416 === (confidence 0.99)
leld a social in Tokyo and invited about 204 Chinese students studying
in Japan. After the social, they visited the Chinese embassy and con-
ferred with the Chinese embassador, who is scheduled to return to
Nanking shortly.
A year has passed since the National Support Society was
organized and today, October 12, is its first anniversary. To com-
memorate this anniversary, the National Support Society held a simple
ceremony today.
An electrical method to investigate underground water has
been devised by .........Iwata, of the electrical experiment division
of the Communication Ministry. In the past, holes had to be dug in
order to investigate under-ground water and this method took a long
time. By the new method, under-ground water can be investigated
without digging holes.
With representatives of England, America and Chungking
finally gatherod in Hongkong, the economic conference will shortly
be started. This conference will result in nothing other than Amer-
ica strengthening her hands in Chungking, it is rumored.
According to a dispatch from Berlin, all the newspapers in
Germany are regarding the conclusion of the trade agreement between
Turkey and Germany as the greatest event of the week. They are
regarding it an an indication that Turkey, though not militarily,
will cooperate with Germany economically in the creation of a new
order in Europe.
The Italian political circles are regarding the recent
coup d'etat of Panama as America's aggressive policy. Their senti-
ment is that the aim of President Roosevelt is to lead America into
the war and in order to do this, he is advocating the nullification
of the Neutrality Law and has even demanded of Panama the arming of
her merchant ships. However, due to the fact that the Panamanian
government rejocted this demand, America instigated the coup d'etat
and brought about the reorganization of the government; and when we
consider what America did to separate Colombia from Panama in order
to protect herPanama Canal and her present hand in the coup d'etat,
we can see the nature of America's aggressive policy clearly.
According to a dispatch from London,........a weekly maga-
zine, published in London, criticized the economic relations between
England and merica, as follows: Taking advantage of the necessity
of England to receive aid from America, American industrialists have
....... the market of England. Besides, it is only proper for America
to aid England, because England is fighting for America's sake. In-
fluential men of London are calling Harriman a Red who is appraising
our homes, and that is an appropriate remark.

News:
According to reports from various quarters, the spearhead
of the German troops reportedly have reached Podolsk. In view of
the fact that the German troops have concluded their campaign in the
south on October 1l and also their campaign in the neighboring regions
of Vyazma and Bryansk, it is believed that their whole strength will
be turned toward Moscow. Consequently, it is believed that they will
reach their objective within two or three weeks.
According to a dispatch from British sources, the greater
part of the Soviet Government has been evacuated and Stalin, too,
reportedly has fled from Moscow.
According to a special communique issued by the German
High Command on October 11, the German troops have routed the Russian
troops in the neighboring regions of Vyazma and Bryansk and are head-
ing toward Moscow. Even the Russian High Command has admitted the
retreat of the Russian troops from Bryansk. The number of Russians
captured in this area is believed to be over 200,000.
According to a dispatch from the front, the Japanese
troops have started a new offensive against the Chinese troops
striving to recapture Ichang. The Japanese troops reportedly have en-
circled the Chinese troops and are reportedly tightening the encircle-
ment. The achievements of the Japanese troops in this area from Sep-
tember 28 to October 5 are, as follows: Killed 7,492 enemy troops,
captured 285 Chinese troops and many arms and ammunition.
According to a dispatch from the front, the Japanese
troops operating in Shangtung Province reportedly have encircled the
Chinese Red Army operating near the border of Shangtung and Kiangsu
and are threatening to annihilate the entire army. The achievements
of the Japm ese troops to date include 371 killed, 93 captured, be-
sides the capture of 209 rifles and many arms and ammunition.
To increase the knowledge of the Japanese people in re-
gard to the South Seas, a South Seas exhibition will be held at the
Mitsukoshi Department Store in Tokyo from October 17 to November 2
under the auspkces of the Nippon Takushoku Kyokai (Japan Colonization
Association). Customs and other things of the South Sea Islands,
Thailand, Malaya, Philippines, Australia and French Indo-China will
be shown.
According to a dispatch from Saigon, the Chinese delega-
tion to establish a trade bureau in Saigon arrived in Saigon today
from Hanoi.
On board a ship, which arrived in Moji yesterday, 16
Japanese evacuees from British Burma returned and stated as follows:
Lately, great many Australian troops have been landed and British
planes have been seen flying almost daily. The people of Burma, in

=== Page 419 === (confidence 0.99)
eneral, are pro-Japanese and are eager to hear news about Japan.
Although they went to alienate themselves from England and America,
they are easily misled by propaganda because they are not well in
formed of Japan. Consequently, England and America busily propagan-
dizing through the radio and motion pictures.
According to a dispatch from London, two conflicting
opinions are being voiced in England. That is, the leftist group is
urging immediate military aid to Soviet Russia, while the conserva-
tive (?) group is advocating the withholding of military aid until
such time as England is fully prepared.
According to a DNB report, the German air force on Octo-
ber 11 and 12 raided Leningrad and Moscow and also took part in the
campaign along the shores of Azov. The number of planes they shot
down from October 11 to October 12 is 101 planes.  On the other hand,
the German air force sank five British freighters, totalling 12,000
tons.

News:
The Japanese troops encircled and annihilated 30,000
Chinese troops in the mountainous region east of Ichang, Hupeh
Province, yesterday. As a result, the Chinese troops, which were
concentrated at a point west of Ichang, lost their fighting spirit
and started retreating northwestwardly. However, the Japanese air
force pursued and inflicted serious damage upon the retreating
force.
According to an announcement made by the Japm ese High
Command in Central China, Chiang Kai-shek, in an attempt to regain
the confidence of the third powers, ordered the Chinese 5th and 6th
divisions to recapture Ichang. However, the commanders of the said
divisions, knowing full well the futility of their task, dispatched
only a part of their divisions toward Ichang, and made a false report
of their achievement to Chungking to save their neck. Hence, Chiang
Kai-shek is trying to regain the confidence of the third powers with
his false propaganda.
Although diplomatic negotiation for the restoration of
friendly relations between Japan and America is being carried on,
America's policy toward the Far East hax not been changed. It has
continued the aid to Chiang and the strengthening of the economic
pressure against Japan. In regard to this, the Tokyo Nichi-Nichi of
today. published an editorial, in which she manifested Japan's
determination and urged the reconsideration of America, as follows:
So long as America clings to the out-modelled Nine Power Treaty, the
Japanese-American negotiation will fall through. The new situation
of East Asia recognizes the necessity for the amendment of the Nine-
Power Treaty. The creation of a new order in East Asia is a product
of this new situation. Moreover, it is the crystallization of the
sweat and blood of 140,000 soldiers who have died, and 1,000,000
officers and men. It is also a noble task, for which our country
has made a great sacrifice. So long as America is blind to this
reality, no prediction as to the future of the Japm ese-American
negotiation can be made. Should America continue her hostile pro-
Chiang acts, Japan will be compelled to assume a grave determination
to cope with the situation.
The Communication Ministry and the Information Bureau
today simultaneously announced the consummation of a treaty between
Jap an and Portugal yesterday. According to this treaty, an airway
will be established between Palao and Deli, Timor, a distance of
2,500 kilomters. The establishment of this airway will draw the
South Sea Islands closer to Japan.

Carrying 539 Japanese evacuees from Malaya, the Osaka
Shosen liner, Fuso Maru, today arrived in Moji. According to the
passengers, the port of Singapore is completely laid with mines and
even the streets are lined with pill-boxes. Consequently, the city
is like a fortress. The day before the departure of the ship, a mine
broke loose and caused considerable confusion. Furthermore, when
members of the crew went ashore and returned to the ship, they were
compelled to t ake off their clothing and shoes and undergo a thorough
examination. Besides, airplanes, making practice flights, often flew
close to the ship and maneuvered about as though in a battle. At
night, they dropped bombs and these bombs often dropped close to the
ship and caused great columns of water to spout.
According to a dispatch from New York, Stslin repor-
tedly has shifted a part of the Russia Far Eastern force to the
European front and is also sending factory workers to the front in
order to stem the German drive. Besides, although Stalin has re-
quested military aid from England, Engl nd is only perfunctorily
raiding German occupied territories in spite of the fact that she has
about 4,000,000 men under arms. Consequently, Russia's indignation
toward England is rising day by day.
According to a Helsinki report, a large number of
British troops has been landed at Arkhangelsk. If this is true, this
is the first time the British troops have been landed on the Ruropean
continent since the beginning of the Russo-German war, but it is
being regarded as merely Britain's gesture toward Russia in view of
Stalin's reproaches against Britain. Canadian troops reportedly are
among those that have been landed at Arkhangelsk.
According to a dispatch from Washington, the foreign
affairs committee of the lower House held a public hearing yesterday
in regard to the nullification of the neutrality law. Those who tes-
tified before the committee and stressed the necessity for the nul-
lification of the said law, vacated their seats in the midst of the
hearing. This is being regarded as their gesture of protest against
the attempts of the majority party to make the hearing a semi-public
hearing. Consequently, considerable criticism is being voiced against
the government's attempt to nullify this law so high-handedly. Further
more, Sen. Wheeler yesterday expressed his objection against the re-
pealing of the neutrality law and the arming of merchant ships, as
follows: "The arming of merchant ships is nothing but a measure
that will invite attacks rather than assist in protection. If Presi-
dent Roosevelt's intention is not to aggravato the prèsent war, then,
I find it hard to understand why he is attempting to arm merchant
ships. Besides, in regard to the priority of war materials, consider-
able dissatisfaction is being voiced within the industrial circles."
According to a dispatch from Bangkok, Wanitto (Phonetic)
has been acquitted of the charge that he is a traitor who has enabled
Japan to make considerable profits by the court of inquiry. It is
believed that England, attempting to drive Wenitto out of office, had
spread the false rumor: of Wanitto's traitorous act.

Comment on current events:
Topic "A review of the week's diplomatic trend". To-
day, our country, under the great ideal of universal concordia, is
making a sturdy stride toward the creation of a mutual prosperity
sphere in East Asia. In Burope, Germany and Italy are striving day
and night to create a new order in Europe, too. What Japan is
aiming at in East Asia, and Germany and Italy in Europe, is nothing
other than the establishment of real world peace based on righteous-
ness. Against this, Britain and America are making every effort to
protect the crumbling democratic array. In Europo, this is being
manifested as American aid to Britain and Russia in the war between
Germany and Italy against Britain and Russia. In the Far East, she
is carrying on various activities, such as encouraging Chungking,
Dutch East Indies, Burma and Malaya to form the ABCD anti-Japanese
encirclement. In this manner, the struggle between the old and the
new is being furthered in a complicated manner in the East and the
West, and it appears as if the new order is making a steedy gain over
the old order. Nevertheless, there appear, at times, signs as
though the old order is pressing the new order, but we should not be
misled by such signs,but should grapp hold of the correct flow of
history. This knowledge is necessary to look at the situation in
East Asia. Although your attention is probably cast upon the transi-
tion of the situation of the world, especially of East Asia, from
the old to the new, we have decided to review the diplomatic happen-
ings of the week every week at this time to give you an accurate
picture of the ever changing and complic ted situation. The impor-
tant happenings of this week are the recent great developments of
Russo-German war, the economic conference of Britain, America and
Chungking at Hongkong, following the British-American conference at
Manila, and the development of America's aggressive Central and South
American policy. Now, let us begin with the Russo-German war. It
was thought that the Russo -German war might pass over the winter in a
stale-mated state, but contrary to this general prediction, a great
offensive against Moscow was started this week. In regard to the
morale of the German troops, it can be readily perceived by the
statement made by.............., director of Germany's newspaper
bureau (?), on October 9 to the effect that this new offtnsive of
Germany will wipe out the last strength of the Soviet troops and will
make Soviet Russia no longer an existing enemy and also by the pro-
clamation issued by Chencellor Hitler to the officers and men of the
German army to the offect that we must destroy the last strength of
the Red Army before winter comes and this offenaive certainly will
crown with glory the achievements we have achieved thus far, for
Soviet Russia will be absolutely destroyed militarily by this final
offensive of ours. As expected, this ignited the morale of the whole
army, and reports of German superiority along all fronts are being
received. That is, Gen. Budenny's army in the south has been com-
pletely annihilated alongthe shores of the Sea of Azov, while Gen.
Voroshilov's army in the north is being ......... in Leningrad. In
the central sector, Gen. Timoshenko's army has been driven back from
Bryansk, Vyazma, and Orel. Hence, the German troops are now only
about 40 kilometers away from Moscow. In this area, 350,000 Russian
troops reportedly have been captured and an order for the evacua tion
-3-

=== Page 423 === (confidence 0.99)
of women and children reportedly ha been issued in Moscow already.
Thus, it can be seen that the fate of Moscow is like a candle
flickering in the wind. In view of defeat of Soviet Russia in
Europe and the decisive defeat of Chungking in East Asia, Britain
and America are extremely perturbed. In England, the parliament is
critieizing the passive nature of Churchill's cabinet's aid to Soviet
Russia and is demanding the formation of a new cabinet. At the same
time, the opinion advocating the landing of British troops on the
continent to ass.ist Russia has come to the fore. However, the
British gtovernment has revealed that it would be impossible to under-
take such a task with her present military strength. If the German
troops continue to maintain their prcsent superiority, it will have
a considerable effect upon the situation of the world, to say nothing
of its military effect. Conseguently, Britain and America are regard-
ing the outcome of the Russo-German war with a desperate look. This
is because the method of aid discussed at the Moscow conference would
become useless depending on tho developments of the coming week or
two. Besides, it is much more obvious than looking at a fire that a
friction will result in the relations between Britain and America, and
Soviet Russia, should Britain and America's aid to Soviet Russia be
unable to keep abreast of the fast progress of the German troops towerd
Moscow. Furthermore, who can tell whether Russia's promise not to
conclude a separate peace vith Germany, together with her other
promises made in the Anglo-Russian military pact, will be kept. What
is more, it has become impossible to ignore the indirect effect of
the outcome of tho Russo-German war upon East Asia, especially upon
the present Pacific problems. Hcrein lies the great significance of
the outcome of the Russo-German war. This is the reason why the
nations of the world are seriously regarding the developments of the
Russo-German war from thcir respective positions. Nextly, let us
consider the Eritain-America-Chungking economic confcrence held at
Hongkong following the Manila conference. This conference was held
with such persons as Henry Grady and Hope as leaders. The subject of
discussion, without doubt, was the strengthening of Chungking's
economy through Britain's and America's aid. Since it is obvious
that no agreement can be reached between these three, whose position
differs, It is only proper to regard that America wiil eventually
come to hold complete control of Chungking. Furthermore, it has
beon reported that an American military missi on has reached Chungking
and is drawing up some sort of scheme. As indicated by Britain's and
America's Manila conference and their Hongkong conference, their Far
Eastern policy is becoming more and more positive. However, as I
have previously explained, this can be regarded as their move to con-
ceal their confusion caused by Sovlet Russia's dcfeat in the war.
With their anxiety in regard to Germany's offensive against Britain
and in the Near East after her decisive victory in the Russo-German
war increasing all the more, it is not hard to presume that Britain
and America, in their attempt to maintain the peace on the Pacific,
will inevitably mitigate their past anti-Japanese intimidation and
threats and attempt somc sly diplomatic negotiation. Even if this is
reflected in the present negotiation between Jann and America and
some tempting offor is made, our country, which Is striving for the
accomplishment of the Incident end the creation of a new order in East
Asia, will not be swayed, but will carefully watch the developments
and strive for the extablishment of peace in East Asia as well as in

the world. Lastly, let us consider the other important happenings.
In Panama, a pro-American coup d'etat was carried out and resulted in
the formation of a new cabinet. This is being regarded as America's
doing because of Panama's rejection of America's demand to arm her
chant ships. Furthermore, a loan agreement reportedly has been
signed between Brazil and America. Both of these are descriptive
manifestation of America's Central and South American policy. How-
ever, since 13 of the .......... of the Central and South American
nations are of German origin and are all politically and economically
powerful, the future of America's Central and South American policy
cannot necessarily be said to be safe.

that the only cherry tree. that blossons three times a year is now in
full.bloom. In Hiroshima Prefecture, it was reported that 3C youths

News:
The final papers settling the border between Outer
Mongolia and Manchouluo were signed today, October 15, at Harbin,
where representatives of both countries had been conferring since
September 23. A statement in regard to the settlement of this border
was issued by the foreign office of Manchoukuoan government today.
The War Ministry today announced its annual shake-up
of personnel. According to the shake-up, Lt. Gen. Shigemitsu (?)
Tanaka, provost-marshal, has been appointed to commander-in-chief of
the Eastern Defense Force. As his successor, Lt. Gen. ......Nakamura,
formerly head of the military and administrative bureaus of the War
Ministry, has been appointed to the post of provost-marshal. Col.
Mabuchi, head of the information bureau of the Imperial Military
Headquarters, has been appointed as commander of a certain detachment.
As his successor, Col. .......Ohira, instructor at the military aca-
demy, has been anpointed. Furthermore, Col. Sato and Chiba (?) have
been promoted to major-generals.
In regard to the Japanese-American negotiation, the
newspapers of Tokyo commented upon it today and expressed their
opinion that a Japenese-American negotiation is of no use, so long as
America continues to maintain her hostile attitude on one hand and
attempts to negotiate on the other hand.
The Autumn festival of the Yasukuni Shrine will be
held from tomorrow. As a prelude to the festivity, 15,00o souls of
the soldiers who had fought at the front were enshrined today.
As has been reported previously, the Japanese govern-
ment has decided to dispatch the Tatsuta Maru, the Mikawa Maru and
Taiyo Maru to America to resume passenger sorvice between the two
countries. The first of these three ships, Tatsuta Maru, left for
San Francisco by way of Honolulu at 5 p.m. today from Yokohama.
The third transfer of warships from the Japanese navy
to the Chinese navy was conducted this afternoon at Canton. Three
warships were transferred today, bringing the total number of war-
ships thus far transferred to nine.
The Osaka Shosen liner, Fuso Maru, carrying on board
362 evacuees from Singapore, arrived in Kobe yesterdey. The evacuees
are all indignant about the treatment accorded them by the British
authorities at Singapore.

Comment on current events:
Topic "A foreslght of the Russo-German War":
It was nothing but German pronaganda voiced through the mouth of a
Japanese. The greater part of the talk was spent in outlining the
achievements accomplished thus far by the German troops. Toward the
end of the talk the aim of Hitler was explained as the complete
annihilation of the Russian army, instead of the capture of Moscow.
This, it was explained, would prevent Russia from carrying on a
guerrilla warfare, thus enabling Germany to withdraw her army from
that area and strike against Britain with renewed vigor. 80 per cent
of this aim, according to the commentator, has been accomplished and
the end should come in about two or three weeks. In this event, the
commentator stated, the fall of England will be but a matter of time.
Hence, England and America are greatly perturbed. In the first
place, the commentator explained, England and America's aid was not
for the purpose of helpiig Russia to win the war, but to contribute
toward the exhaustion of the Russian and the German armies through
the protraction of the war. In view of their failure to achieve this

=== Page 427 === (confidence 0.97)
aim, the commentator concluded that America. eventually will be com-
pelled to take part in the war on the side of England.

News:
At today's cabinet meeting, plans for the controlling
of funds and exports and imports to Manchoukuo and China were decided.
The empcror yesterday sent a message of congratulation
to the president of Mexico on the anniversary of its independence.
Manchoukuo yesterday observed the 10th anniversary of
the founding of the country with various kinds of celebrations.
Japanese astronomers at Hankow, Keelung, and ......
have completed their preparation for the observation of the total
eclipse of the sun on September 21.
A report has been received stating that the 32 men,
who left Yokohama on July 17 to salvage the Arima Maru, which went
aground near Marinto Island (phonetic) off the coast of Peru during
the middle part of May, succeeded in refloating the said shin yester-
day, September 15.
According to a statement of the German High Command
issued yesterday, the Soviet troops have built pill-boxes at every
important point around Leningrad and besides mining the area in front
of the pili-boxes, they have dug tank traps, two meters deep and
three meters wide, to prevent the progress of German tanks. However,
the German troops have already surmounted the majority of these
defense obstacles and are bombing the important facilities wi thin the
city with long range guns. Besides, cooperating with the Finnish
troops, who are advancing southward from the north, the German troops
have already driven the 4,500,000 people of Leningrad into an area of
5,00o kilometers square. Hence, the fall of Leningrad is now a matter
of time.
According to a communique issued by the German High
Command, the German troops have succeeded in breaking through the
first line of defense of the Russian troops at Leningrad, thus en-
dangering the fate of Leningrad. Along the other fronts, the German
troops are also making steady progress. In regard to this, a dispatch
from London admits the cutting off of the railway lines from Chernigov
to Kiev and from Kremenchug to Kiev and the crossing of the Dnepr
River by the Germans in the south. Besides, the Soviet authorities
admitted the abandonment of Kremenchug.
According to a DNB report of September 15, the German
air force on September 15 destroyed a 5,000 ton Russian transport at
Odessa, besides damaging another merchant ship of about 3,Ooo tons.
